[03:05:44] Our primary objective is to design and construct a new fire and rescue station that will serve as a replacement for the current Philomont Volunteer Fire and Rescue Company No. 8 station. The new station will be built on a seven-acre site located along Snickersville Turnpike.

[03:06:02] The property is currently owned by the Philomont Volunteer Fire Company and will be donated to the county at no cost. Another objective was to ensure that the new station is appropriately integrated into the rural setting of Philomont. [05:47:55] Good evening, Madam Chair. My name is Jacob Pambrick. I'm a senior planner with Zoning Administration within the Department of Planning and Zoning, and I am the project manager for ZOAM 2020-0002, Prime Agricultural Soils and Cluster Subdivisions.

[05:48:10] This evening, I will briefly touch upon the purpose and the background of the ZOAM, as well as the public outreach efforts and proposed draft text changes and key issues for the discussion. The purpose of the ZOAM is to amend the Loudoun County Zoning Warrants in order to preserve prime agricultural soils, soils, improve cluster development design, and ensure the continued success of rural economy uses in the Agricultural Rural 1 and Agricultural Rural 2 zoning

[05:48:43] districts in the rural policy area. On June 16, 2020, a board member initiative was brought forward and approved seeking to amend the zoning ordinance regulations pertaining to cluster development design, preservation of prime agricultural soils, and to ensure the success of rural economy uses in the AR1 and AR2 zoning districts the amendments are intended to improve the design of cluster residential development by incorporating natural features protecting and conserving agriculturally productive prime agricultural soils align allowing for equine and rural economy uses and further implementing the clustered or

[05:49:22] residential development policies of the 2019 general plan to guide all future clustered sub subdivisions applications in the AR1 and AR2 zoning district Following approval of the BMI, staff presented an updated ZOAM work plan to the board.

[05:49:39] The board approved the updated ZOAM work plan that directed staff to develop amendments to the cluster subdivision regulations in the AR1 and AR2 zoning districts as a separate standalone ZOAM independent of the zoning ordinance rewrite effort. At the July 21st, 2021 TLUC meeting, staff presented an information item that reported out the comments received from the initial outreach sessions.

[05:50:05] TELUC directed staff to hold additional outreach sessions with those familiar with conservation easements in order to identify potential impacts of the ZOAM to include a decrease in development potential and an increase in the development costs that could result in a reduction in the amount of land being placed in conservation easements.

[05:50:23] At the June 21st, 2022 board business meeting, the board adopted the resolution of intent to amend to formally initiate ZOAM 2020-0002. As part of the vote to adopt the resolution of intent to amend, staff was directed to prepare an analysis that evaluated whether the proposed amendments may create a disincentive for property owners to place parcels and conservation easements.

[05:50:49] And the analysis include, but not be limited to, consultation with private conservation easement appraisers. the Commissioner of Revenue, and other experts in the conservation easement field. Prior to drafting the text of the ZOAM, Zoning Administration staff conducted 12 outreach sessions between February 2021 and August 2021, which generated over 350 comments from over 150 participants.

[05:51:18] Based on the comments received from all 12 outreach groups, staff prepared the initial draft text as well as other subsequent draft texts. Although there were 12 individual stakeholder groups, as well as the county agencies and the Zoning Ordinance Committee, providing comments to the ZOAM, there were several key topics that were echoed throughout the different groups.

[05:51:41] Some of the main topics include the characteristics of the rural economy lot, design characteristics of the cluster subdivision, the protection of prime farmland soils, and the amount that should be preserved, uses that are appropriate on prime farmland soils and lot types within cluster subdivisions, as well as potential impacts the ZOAM could have on conservation easements going forward.

[05:52:01] The key issues guided much of the draft text as provided with the staff report as attachment one. Following the input and review by the Zoning Wardens Committee, staff presented the ZOAM draft text to the Planning Commission at their November 14, 2022 public hearing.

[05:52:20] The public hearing was followed by four Planning Commission work sessions from January 2023 to December 2023, three of which were public work sessions, which allowed public comment due to a change in the Virginia State Code. During the time between the four work sessions, a work group was appointed by the board to work through the key issues identified through the previous stakeholder outreach efforts and formulate revisions to the proposed ZOAM text.

[05:52:46] As a result of the work group, several changes to the ZOAM text were incorporated into the current draft text. In addition to the work group, at the September 14, 2023 Commission work session, staff presented the consultant analysis completed by Kim Lee Horn, which analyzed potential impacts of the draft ZOAM text on future AR1 and AR2 cluster subdivisions and future conservation easement efforts. Based on the analysis, recommendations were provided by the consultant that could lessen the potential impact of the draft ZOAM text on future cluster subdivisions,

[05:53:22] particularly the preservation of prime farming soils. These recommendations can be found within the staff report. The draft ZOAM text reviewed and forwarded by the Planning Commission was under the format and organizational style of the revised 1993 Loudoun County Zoning Ordinance.

[05:53:41] As part of the approved commission motion, staff was directed to reformat the draft text language to be consistent with the zoning ordinance format approved by the board at the December 13, 2023 board public hearing. The new zoning ordinance was substantially reorganized and rewritten to facilitate structure and flow. Therefore, in the process of reformatting the draft zoan text revisions have been made to the applicable sections of the zoning ordinance for consistency clarity ease of use and to remove redundancy although the draft text requirements now appear within several chapters and sections of the zoning ordinance the requirements remain the

[05:54:18] same as recommended by the commission a crosswalk document is provided as attachment nine of the staff report which references where proposed requirements are located within the zoning ordinance in relation to where they were located in the revised 1993 Loudoun County Zoning Ordinance.

[05:54:35] With that being said, the proposed amendments to Chapter 2, specifically the cluster option within the AR1 and AR2 zoning districts, included changes to the cluster option's dimensional standards, such as setbacks and building requirements, as well as new requirements for the new preservation farm lot type. Updates to Chapter 3 uses include changes to the use permissions for each cluster subdivision lot type. The proposed draft text lists out the different lot types of the cluster subdivision with each lot type having specific uses listed as either

[05:55:10] permitted by right or by minor special exception or special exception. Table 3.02.03-1 of the zoning ordinance lists all of the uses in the zoning ordinance and specifies which uses are permitted by right or by special exception within the AR1 and AR2 zoning districts. Some of the The changes included the limitations of uses on the new preservation farm lots to those that are associated with agriculture or benefit from the prime farmland soils, given the main purpose of the preservation farm lot is to preserve prime agricultural soils.

[05:55:45] Use permissions changes were also updated for the rural economy cluster lot type, which included removing higher intense uses, which typically generate increased traffic and noise that may adversely impact residents within a cluster subdivision. The use-specific standards of Chapter 4 were amended to specify the use permissions for the various lot types in the cluster subdivision option.

[05:56:08] For each use listed in the use table of Chapter 3, a reference to the use-specific standard is listed if applicable. Updates to Chapter 7, development standards included a minor change to Section 7.02 open space, indicating that the open space requirements are not required for cluster subdivision option, and a note added referencing back to the cluster subdivision option requirements of Chapter 2.

[05:56:35] As part of Chapter 12, the applicable current definitions were revised and or deleted, as well as new definitions established, such as common open space lot, preservation farm lot, prime farmland soils, residential cluster lot, and rural economy cluster lot.

[05:56:56] Specific uses related to the drafts OM text were identified during the interagency referral process, meetings with ZOC, and community outreach sessions with specific stakeholder groups. These topics were also extensively discussed throughout the planning commission process. Those key issues included amount of prime farmland soils present on an originating track to require a preservation farm lot, percentages of prime farmland soils to be preserved, permitted, and special exception uses on specific lot types.

[05:57:25] Regarding the amount of prime farmland soils present on an originating track to require a preservation farm lot, Table 1 on page 14 of the staff report shows the various amounts of contiguous and non-contiguous prime farmland soils and the possible total preserved prime farmland soils acreage based on different required preservation percentages.

[05:57:46] Staff has also evaluated the numbers based on a 10-acre non-contiguous option that was not included in the staff report. Staff notes that the current draft text reflects the requirement of five acres non-contiguous as to when a preservation farm lot is required.

[05:58:04] The updated table will be shown on the following slide. For the percentage of farmland soils to be preserved within preservation farm lots, the current draft text reflects a 70% preservation. The proposed uses on the specific cluster subdivision lot types forwarded by the Planning Commissioner are unchanged.

[05:58:23] However, given the reformatting of the draft text to the format of the current zoning ordinance, some uses were renamed or were combined into another use. For example, ecotourism under the revised 1993 Loudoun County Zoning Ordinance now under the cultural tourism use as provided in the current zoning ordinance another example would be cross-country skiing which is now part of the recreation outdoor major use under the current zoning ordinance also to further clarify the proposed uses only apply to those proposed

[05:58:55] lot types within the cluster subdivision option of the ar1 and ar2 zoning district for reference here's the table updated table one which now includes the total for a 10 acre non-contiguous option. This option lists a total of 537 parcels with a total acreage of 48,547 with 17,116 acres being prime farmland soils. The Planning Commission recommended the the two saving clauses proposed by

[05:59:30] staff and the consultant to be to be part of the draft text. The proposed draft savings clauses are provided to help ensure that 30 percent of the originating tract will be available for placement of residential cluster lots, which help address concerns that requiring 70% preservation of prime farmland soils within an originating tract will have an impact on reducing the amount of land that will be placed in conservation easements due to a possible reduction in the number of residential cluster lots. This approach allows for flexibility on sites that have several regulatory constraints and or sporadic pockets of prime farmland soils that could limit the

[06:00:10] development potential of the originating tract. Additionally, staff notes that the 30% of the originating tract is for those residential cluster lots and those uses that are permitted by right or by special exception within those residential cluster lots. To further explain the savings clauses, an applicant can request an administrative reduction if it can be demonstrated that the originating tract, either one, has less than 30% available for the development of residential cluster lots due to the requirement to preserve 70 percent prime farmland soils on site with other

[06:00:46] regulatory impacts and physical site constraints or two the amount of prime farmland soils are less than 15 percent of the originating tract and are distributed in smaller isolated pockets throughout the site that would result in the preservation of 70 percent of the prime farmland soils on a preservation farm lot which could have an impact on the ability to reach the development potential for the residential cluster lots.

[06:01:11] If an administrative reduction is received, it would be processed as a zoning determination in accordance with Section 10.2 of the zoning ordinance. The request must contain an engineered or non-engineered plat showing the location of all prime farmland soils and those areas to be preserved, the size and location of all prime farmland soils and those areas to be preserved, the size and location area of the proposed development area and all other applicable site features that may preclude development. A narrative must also be included summarizing the request

[06:01:47] and the justification for the reduced preservation percentage. Once received it is determined that and it is determined that all the required information is provided. The zoning administrator will consult with appropriate county subject matter experts to assist in determining whether a reduction in the present in the percentage of the required prime farm land soils is warranted based on the submitted documentation of facts and the justification provided by the applicant. If it is deemed necessary, then an outside consultant is needed to assist in evaluating the request. The zoning administrator would seek an engineering professional

[06:02:24] or firm not currently providing the same or similar engineering design or services for the Loudon-based parcels. Staff notes that as part of the administrative reduction request, the zoning administrator cannot reduce the required preservation percentage of the prime farmland soils to less than 30 percent. Once the zoning determination is issued, the applicant or agreed party will have the opportunity to appeal the zoning determination to the Board of Zoning Appeals if so inclined. As pointed out in the staff report, two items were identified as points of discussion. First, general open space maintenance. While open space lots are not a requirement

[06:03:01] of the cluster subdivision option within the zoning ordinance, section 7.02G lists requirements pertaining to the maintenance of open space areas. Staff believes this requirement would be beneficial if applied to any common open space lot created within a cluster subdivision. Staff brings this to the attention of the board for direction as maintenance requirements for open space lots within the cluster subdivision option was not part of the ZOAM draft text reviewed and forwarded to the board at the commission's December 14, 2023 public hearing. Secondly, short-term residential commercial whole house use within the cluster subdivision option.

[06:03:38] The board adopted ZOAM 2018-0001 short-term residential rentals at the April 4th, 2023 board business meeting to allow short-term residential and commercial whole house rentals within certain zoning districts to include the AR1 and AR2 zoning districts.

[06:03:57] Within the current AR1 and AR2 zoning districts, the uses are permitted by right. Although the board approved ZOAM 2018-001, these uses were not included within the draft text use tables for the ZOAM. which were reviewed and forwarded by the commission at the December 14, 2023 commission work session.

[06:04:19] Therefore, staff seeks guidance from the board as to whether these uses should be permitted within the cluster subdivision option. The planning commission forwarded ZOAM 2020-002 to the board with a recommendation of approval that language be added to provide two administrative reduction saving clauses, options for the amount of prime farmland soils to be preserved within the preservation farm lots, and that the proposed draft text be consistent with the zoning ordinance format.

[06:04:48] The draft text as forwarded by the Planning Commission is provided for within Attachment 1 of the staff report. And finally, staff supports board approval of the draft text for ZOAM 2020-0002 as provided in Attachment 1.
